- [x] **EMBED-1 · Drishya inside a harness app's side window: design and contract** · P0 · `rel` `ui` — Yash, 14 Nov. Harness apps with their own desktop, web and mobile apps (for example T3 Code: open source, with desktop, web, iOS and Android apps) want people in their own app, and may replicate what Drishya does. "if we make it easy to integrate as well as in some way in the app itself, people wont go for replicating and having a cost to do same but just adapt directly as a free option and get community support as well." Many of these apps have an artifact or browser window: "like a click run the drishya for it in the second window and users can do a full screen." "this i feel should be in the first target." The CLI and the terminal launch stay for people who want only Drishya; VS Code and other editor extensions come later.
  - **Contract:** [embed-1-in-app.md](review/embed-1-in-app.md), drafted by Codex; Yash confirmed both builders agree on 14 Nov. Release 1 uses top-level panes only (iframe flag refused), a one-use launch handoff, and a 360 px embed-only minimum. Implementation is EMBED-2a/2b. The original six-point brief follows:
    1. **What the app opens:** a URL for one session (an embed route), shown in the app's browser or artifact pane, with a full-screen control. The app starts Drishya through a documented command or package, or finds it already running.
    2. **How the click happens:** a button the app adds, or the model itself through a small launcher (an MCP tool or a slash command) that starts Drishya and returns the URL the pane opens. Read-only by default; the composer only where Drishya's controller is already allowed for that harness.
    3. **Security:** loopback-only binding and the request gate still hold inside another app's webview. Framing is allowed only for what the host declares. No token appears in a URL a page could read. SEC-3's suite covers the embed.
    4. **Size:** side panes are often narrower than 600 px. The embed's minimum width is a builder decision, because it changes the desktop-only rule (26 Oct). Mobile apps (iOS, Android) are not in Release 1: Drishya runs on the computer that holds the sessions.
    5. **Free and supported:** Apache-2.0 (PUBLIC-1), an integration guide, a small working example, and the community channel.
    6. **First reference integration:** T3 Code, as it is open source. The builders propose it to its maintainers; an agent never does.
  - **Accept:** the contract in `docs/review/embed-1-in-app.md`, answering the six points, agreed by Yash and Harsh. **Done, 14 Nov:** six decisions, auth and SEC-3 matrix, pinned T3 Code source and implementation boundaries recorded; both-builder agreement confirmed by Yash in chat. Docs-only acceptance, not runtime/security certification.

Not a full-screen view and not a new layout: the target is that **the agent, or one command, opens the current session in the app's own browser or artifact pane.**
  - **Shape:** a `/drishya` slash command or skill (Claude Code, Codex), and the MCP tool `drishya.open_view` from #497, resolve the current session (its id where the harness exposes it, else `--latest --cwd`), run `drishya embed`, and the agent opens the returned URL in its pane: the Claude desktop browser pane, the Codex app's pane, a T3 Code webview, otherwise the system browser.
  - **What carries over:** #497 (Codex: `drishya embed`, the launch-code exchange, the MCP tool, discovery) is the base. Its PR is closed and its branch `codex/embed-2a` is kept. #499 (Antigravity: the full-screen embed view) is closed and deleted, because it built the wrong target. The EMBED-1 contract stays, except that it assumed a host app writes code; here the agent opens the pane.

- [x] **LIVE-7 · Live latency at every step** · P0 · `live` `perf` — Yash, 11 Nov: measure the delay between the agent's work and Drishya showing it, across every path. The last measurement was about 2 s on a 1,100-operation Codex session.
  - **Yash, 14 Nov, a probable bug to find first:** "sometimes its that the session doesnt load and than next second there is thinking block and 2-3 commands already done." A live session can sit empty or stale, then several steps appear at once. Reproduce it on a real live Claude Code and Codex run (count the gap between journal write and render per step, and the steps that arrive together), find where they are held (the tail, batching, the projection, paging or the UI's live merge), and fix it before tuning the averages.
  - **Yash, 11 Nov: P0.** Every item Yash asked for without saying it was for later is Release 1. Independent of the UI queue: daemon live path and page instrumentation only. PERF-3 stays separate; LIVE-7 measures large journals but does not take on PERF-3's decision.
  - **Do:** instrument, on the daemon and the page, the time from the harness writing a record to Drishya painting it, using the journal's own timestamps and file-append times (no telemetry; local only, behind a developer switch). Measure each step separately: a new session appearing on Home and opening; the prompt after it is sent (from the terminal and from Drishya's composer); a tool call starting; its result; agent prose; a plan or question; the turn ending; a sub-agent starting. Do this for Claude Code and Codex, passive observation and the connected controller, small and 150 MB+ journals. Then shorten the slowest steps (watch intervals, batching, projection work, render scheduling) without changing pixels or motion.
  - **Accept:** a table in `docs/review/` with p50 and p95 per step, harness, path and journal size, before and after; a target agreed with Yash (for example under 500 ms p95 for live steps on ordinary journals); a unit or bench that guards it; capture never waits on presentation.
  - **Done 14 Nov (Claude Sonnet 5.5, PR for Fable's review):** [live-7-latency.md](review/live-7-latency.md). Cause of the bug: Home listed new sessions from a 10 s page poll (9.3 s measured on a real Codex run), a session was followed only once opened, and the page re-scanned every tool-output line on every update. Fixed: source notice over SSE, 100 ms tail with an immediate first read, immediate first refresh, memoised output cards, cached import check, blob memo. Ordinary journals p95 0.16-0.25 s (was 0.58-3.5 s); the proposed target (p95 under 500 ms, ordinary; under 1 s, paged) awaits Yash's agreement. Not met and not started: non-paged sessions of about 3,000+ observations (p95 about 1 s, a whole-projection rebuild per update; an incremental reduce is the next item). Not measured: live Claude Code runs (its sign-in here has expired), sub-agent and `ExitPlanMode` steps.
- [x] **LINK-1 · Hover cards for links in agent prose** · P0 · `content` `git` — Yash, 8 Nov, with a screenshot of Claude Desktop's PR hover card (state, repository, number, title, author, +/− and files). Release 1 if it fits before the freeze; otherwise first after the release.
  - **Yash, 8 Nov:** every URL in agent prose and in web/browser tool cards (web fetch, web search results, browser and MCP browser tools) is clickable and opens in the default browser. A PR or issue opens there too; in-app PR screens come later (VIZ-1).

    3. A GitHub PR or issue shows state, title, author, +/− and files. This comes only through the user's own installed and signed-in `gh` CLI, only after an explicit opt-in in Settings (Drishya sends nothing anywhere by itself, holds no token, and never fetches arbitrary URLs). Results are cached for the session, and without opt-in or `gh` the card says so plainly.
    4. Any other URL shows only its domain and full address; nothing is fetched.
    - The card opens on hover or focus with a short delay and closes with motion (MOTION-4). Clicking a PR opens it in the browser for Release 1; a PR changes screen inside Drishya is VIZ-1.
  - **Accept:** real Claude Code and Codex sessions whose prose contains file paths, a commit, a PR and plain URLs, at 600 and 1440: each card is correct; no network request happens without opt-in (verified in the network log); keyboard focus shows the same card. **Implemented in PR #405, accepted 11 Nov.** Web addresses in agent prose (bare, Markdown links, code spans) and in browser/web tool cards open in the default browser in a new tab; captured documents stay inert. Hover or keyboard focus opens a card: a file (name, folder, captured or current), a commit or local branch (subject, author, date, +/−, files from read-only Git once the session's Git inspection is approved), a GitHub pull request or issue (title, state, author, +/−, files, only through the person's own `gh` after the opt-in in Settings → Links, never without it; the daemon runs nothing and the browser requests nothing before the opt-in) and any other address (domain and full address, nothing fetched). Verified at 600 and 1440 on a genuine Claude Code capture and existing Claude and Codex sessions; Codex commit and branch cards are not covered (no Codex prose names one and Codex could not be run).
- [x] **SHIP-14 · Queued inputs shows only the queue** · P0 · `live` `controller` `ui` — Yash, 8 Nov, with a screenshot: the dialog lists every enqueue receipt of the session (137), each with "item outcome unknown", so it reads as a backlog.
  - **Do:** the list shows only inputs that are still waiting. A receipt leaves the list when its prompt appears in History, a dequeue or removal is recorded, or the session has since gone idle (a later completed turn). Never claim it was delivered unless the matching prompt is in History. Receipts whose outcome cannot be established stay reachable under the existing Earlier receipts disclosure, labelled as history. When nothing is queued, the entry point does not show a count.

Units cover the resolution rules. **Complete for review:** genuine acceptance passes; full-gate failures reproduce on clean main. [Evidence and limits](review/ship-14-pending-queue.md).

### BUG — defects
- [x] **CONTENT-19 · Blob-backed diff lines do not crash the reader** · P0 · `content` `bug` — found during SHIP-14 genuine acceptance, 9 Nov. Claude `f49a9e6d` (3,370,797 bytes) has two sealed blob references among 1,766 hunk lines. At replay step 467, DiffView in `apps/ui/src/components/common.tsx` calls `.slice(1)` on a non-string line and the view falls into ScreenBoundary. Reproduced at 600 px on both the release integration and rebuilt clean main `f93d880b`; no queue or hunk content was printed or changed.
  - **Do:** preserve exact sealed diff material/provenance while loading or honestly representing blob-backed lines before string-only diff rendering. Check the other hunk consumers too; never stringify a blob reference, drop a line, weaken redaction or fabricate source text. Claim before implementing.
  - **Accept:** genuine long-line diff at 600/1440 in History/replay remains readable and selectable with no renderer crash or lost bytes; a sealed long-line fixture guards each affected diff consumer. Private evidence: release integration `.local/release-real/`, clean-main `.local/release1-native-crash/`. **Fixed 10 Nov (PR #387).** The reducer restores sealed hunk lines from redacted storage before any reader sees them (an unreadable blob shows its stored preview with an explicit marker), so every diff consumer gets strings; replay bundles exported by older builds are normalised on load; reducer r38 rebuilds old projections. Verified on a recorded Claude Code session with a 32 KB line at 600 and 1440 in History and replay (the same case crashes History on main); `pnpm check:real -- blob-diff-lines` guards it. No real Codex session has a patch line past 16 KiB and Codex could not be run (usage limit), recorded as not covered.
- [x] **CONTENT-20 · Mid-turn messages are prompts** · P0 · `content` `bug` — Yash, 10 Nov: the prompt rail of a long genuine Claude Code session (`dd136b25`, 171 MB) shows "Prompt N of 121". Opus counted the journal (counts only): 105 person prompts at turn starts, plus 32 person messages sent while the agent was working, recorded as `attachment` entries of type `queued_command` with `origin` human and `commandMode` prompt. That is 137; another 79 queued commands are task notifications and are correctly not prompts.
  - **Do:** find which person messages Drishya drops or merges (expected: the mid-turn `queued_command` human ones, plus any other shape) and count them as person prompts through CONTENT-18's shared classifier. Show each in History at the point it was delivered, as the person's prompt capsule (UI-27). Keep task notifications as notices. Check Codex's equivalent (mid-turn steer input) for the same gap.
  - **Accept:** on `dd136b25` and at least one other genuine Claude session with mid-turn messages: the rail total equals the journal's person turn starts plus human queued commands, Prompt N/M and the overview agree, and each mid-turn message appears once in History at 600 and 1440. A Codex steer session, if one exists locally. Units cover the classification. `check:real` gains the case.
  - **10 Nov, completed (Claude), PR #396:** the cause was not the classifier. The real store held 303 `message.user` observations of `dd136b25` against 356 on a fresh import: 53 `queued_command` rows (14 person, 39 notices, 29 to 31 Oct) consumed before #310 were never re-read. Fix: a one-time per-recording startup rescan (`rescanLegacyClaude`); one prompt per re-emitted journal record (five person rows were duplicated in `dd136b25`; reducer r39); a `<cross-session-message>` from another Claude session is a notice, not a prompt. `check:real -- mid-turn-prompts` passes on 10 of 10 genuine sessions, `dd136b25` snapshot 138 = 106 turn starts + 32 mid-turn; checked at 600 and 1440. Codex steers are ordinary user items (no gap; none local to re-check). [Cause, counts and limits](review/content-20-mid-turn-prompts.md).
- [x] **CONTENT-21 · Continued sessions join their earlier journal** · P0 · `content` `data` — Yash, 10 Nov: both Claude Desktop and Drishya start this coordinating session at 29 Oct, but it began on 19 Oct. Opus found (ids and counts only): original journal `c757fa6a` (234 MB, 19 Oct to 29 Oct 12:10 UTC); on 29 Oct at 12:10 Claude Code continued it as `dd136b25`, copying only the tail of the history from 10:49 UTC with the **same entry uuids** (397 of the new file's first 400 entries also exist in `c757fa6a`), re-stamped with the new sessionId. Nothing else links them.
  - **Do:** extend the existing session-lineage join (the `session-lineage` real case; CONTENT-18 joined two journals for `rec_c8e5c9c8`) to this shape. When a journal's leading entries share uuids with an earlier journal of the same project, treat it as one session: the earlier journal's history first, then the new journal's entries de-duplicated by uuid, so nothing appears twice. The join rests only on that evidence (shared uuids), never on time or title proximity. Prompt numbering, the overview, search and Changes see the joined history. Large joined sessions keep the existing paged loading.
  - **Accept:** `c757fa6a` + `dd136b25` imported into a scratch home: Prompt 1 is the 19 Oct prompt, the total is the sum without duplicates (state counts), and no copied entry appears twice, at 600 and 1440. A second genuine continued pair if one exists. Units cover the de-duplication; `check:real -- session-lineage` gains the case; `package:verify` passes.
  - **10 Nov, completed (Claude), PR #401:** the compact-boundary parent uuid was held by two local journals (`c757fa6a` and a sibling copy of the same tail), so the link was `ambiguous` and the history began on 29 Oct. A candidate parent must now not itself start with entries the child holds (siblings and forks drop out; ancestors of another match drop out), and a journal that copies a tail without a boundary row links by shared entry uuids (`basis: 'shared-entry-uuids'`, a new derived-link value). `dd136b25` + `c757fa6a`: linked, joined 344 person prompts (207 + about 141, copies counted once), first prompt 19 Oct, rail 344 at 600 and 1440. Units; `check:real -- session-lineage` extended. [Cause, counts and limits](review/content-21-continued-journals.md).

Vertical is now `auto` (horizontal stays contained). Verified on Codex and Claude sessions: at a card's top edge the wheel scrolls the history 300 px per step; mid-card scrolling stays local with the history unchanged.

Measured on the AgentGuard Claude session: `.history.scrollTop` stayed at 71627 for two wheel events over a Read card. Cards cover most of the centre, so this happens constantly.

Check the hover-reading and zoom handlers in `lib/hover-reading.ts` and `modern/local-scroll.ts` for `preventDefault` on wheel.

- [x] **BUG-2 · Git review drawer covers the Git graph button; Escape inconsistent** · P0 · `bug` `git` `ui` — **fixed 19 Oct.** The right-docked Git drawer now starts below the measured navigation (`--drawer-top`, `ReadingWindow.tsx`), so Review ⇄ Graph is one click. Measured 0 overlaps at 640/768/820/853/900/1024/1100 px (853 and 1024 stand in for 150% and 125% zoom of a 1280 window); unchanged at 1280+ and ≤ 600 px. **Residual (moved to UI-1):** the small bottom-corner pills (collapsed Tools, Follow live) still float over the edge of prose at narrow widths, and the phone layout was not re-audited. - [x] **BUG-7 · Live refresh during session open drops the restored reader** · P1 · `bug` `live` — **fixed 19 Oct.** `reloadProjection` now waits for an in-flight open of the same recording before taking its token; the open settles once its location is applied, so its own live-start reload cannot wait on itself. New `open-refresh-race.test.ts` failed before and passes; "session detours" passes 10/10 at 150 ms and 10/10 with coalescing forced to 25 ms.
  - **Problem:** `openRecording` (`apps/ui/src/state.ts`) clears the reading location, awaits recordings and projection, then applies the saved location. A live refresh that starts meanwhile (`useLiveUpdates` → `reloadProjection`) takes the newest projection token, so `openRecording` discards its result and the saved reader is never restored. Reproduced every time with 25 ms coalescing; PR128 restored 150 ms, which only makes it unlikely.
  - **Accept:** a unit test reproduces it; `fresh-workspace.spec.ts` "session detours" passes 10/10 alone, also with coalescing temporarily at 25 ms.
- [x] **BUG-8 · Fake file paths from search output** · P1 · `bug` `read` — Yash, 27 Oct: the Files tree shows folders that exist nowhere (`ok 146 tests`, `1) tests`, `[270`, `0-trace.stacks`). **Cause:** `parseHitLines` (`packages/projection/src/shell.ts`) takes everything before `:NN:` as a path, so a `grep` over Playwright output (`ok 146 tests/browser/x.spec.ts:12:1 › title`, `[270/408] tests/browser/…`, `1) tests/…`) registers fake files as search hits; they reach the Files tree, per-file counts, the file timeline and the link graph (`file:<fake path>`), which breaks "never invent a location". **Do:** accept a text-parsed hit only if it is a plausible path (no whitespace in a relative path; an absolute path may contain spaces), a unit test with the exact junk lines, bump the reducer so old recordings re-project, and report counts of fake paths before and after on real local sessions. **Accept:** none of those folders appear for a session that ran such greps; real hits are unchanged.
  - **27 Oct, fixed (reducer r18):** a text-parsed search hit becomes a file reference only if `plausibleHitPath` accepts it (relative paths may not contain whitespace; absolute paths may). The hit itself stays in the search card as captured text; only the file registration, per-file counts, file timeline and link graph change. **Real sessions (counts only):** a static 83 MB Claude session: search-hit paths 215 → 184 (exactly the 31 relative paths with whitespace, 19 distinct, 13 fake top-level folders), real hits unchanged; a live 195 MB Claude session: fake top-level folders 24 → 0, whitespace paths 56 → 0. Unit tests use the exact junk lines. **Known limit:** a real relative file name containing a space is no longer decorated in the tree when it appears only in grep text (it stays in the card); structured search results (`files` lists) are unaffected. Old recordings re-project on the reducer bump. DATA-3 (#206) also sets r18 and takes r19 when it merges.

- [x] **LIVE-1 · "Now" line plus tab title/favicon** · P0 · `live` — **shipped 19 Oct.** While a recording is being watched, the composer pill (and the expanded composer's status) shows one quiet line from captured evidence only: `Claude · running pnpm test · 42 s +1`, `Codex · reading index.ts · 3 s`, `Claude · waiting for your answer · 12 s`, `Codex · working`, `Claude · replied 2 m 05 s ago`, `… · no activity for 40 s`. The tooltip gives the exact basis ("A call is open: the harness recorded it … and no result yet", "This turn so far: 7 operations."). The tab title becomes `● Running · <repo>`, `? Needs you · <repo>` or `◐ Working · <repo>` and the favicon gets a matching dot only while the agent is active; both restore on reply, quiet or leaving the session. No line at all for a session whose newest event is over two hours old. New `GET /v1/recordings/:id/now` (via the history worker, no schema change) returns facts from the **tail** of the recording, so the line stays right while the person is reading older history. Pure logic with an injected clock: `packages/scene/src/now.ts` and `apps/ui/src/lib/now-state.ts` (21 tests). Verified on this very conversation's live Claude session (running → working → running as my own calls opened and closed), on an old session (no line), Home (title and favicon restored), and the expanded composer.

### TURN — turns and navigation

- [x] **TURN-1 · Turn chapters with ledger** · P0 · `turn` — **shipped 19 Oct (header and ledger; collapse split out as TURN-1b).** Each prompt is a chapter: its words clamped to three lines with "Show full prompt" only when it actually overflows, and, once the turn is over, one quiet mono line of what it did, e.g. `edited 28 files (+374 −46) · 31 commands · 18 m 18 s` (parts shown only when non-zero: read, searched, edited files with +/−, commands, tests `41✓ 1✗`, web, MCP, delegated, `N failed` in red). The tooltip says it is counted from captured operations and that the duration runs to the last captured time in the turn (derived; not every harness records where a turn ends). The newest turn shows no ledger while the agent is active (the Now line is live), and replay shows none, so nothing tells the future. Counts come from a new `GET /v1/recordings/:id/turns` (whole recording, so paged sessions are right; small sessions are counted in the browser) built on the pure `packages/scene/src/turns.ts`; passages carry `turnStep` to find their ledger (goldens updated: only the new field). Verified on the 41-turn Codex session (28-file, 18-minute turn) and this live session; clamp 123 px ↔ 345 px expanded. Tests: scene 6, ledger wording 5, daemon route 1.
  - **No schema change.** Codex's recorded `duration_ms` (review 125 S1) would replace the derived duration later. Tests inside Codex `exec` wrappers show as `0` runs until DATA-1.

- [x] **TURN-1b · Collapse finished turns** · P1 · `turn` `motion` — **shipped 20 Oct (Modern only).** The turn's ledger line is the fold control (a chevron turns; no new chrome). A folded turn keeps its prompt and ledger; the rest of its passages become zero-height slots. The composer now marks every passage with `inTurn` (goldens: only the new field), so paged-out members fold too and a paged-out opener still shows as a header. Folds are stored by the opening passage's id (stable while live work arrives) in working locations (`history/folded-turns`, persisted across reloads); the open height is kept separately from the folded one, so unfolding lands where it was. The header never moves: the reading anchor holds, and when folding near the end would shorten the document under the reader, the old extent is held and shrinks as they scroll back. The selected/expanded operation's turn and any prompt-rail jump unfold it (and it stays open); the newest turn cannot fold while the agent is active; replay never folds. Motion: work below the header travels from where it was (260 ms), unfolded work eases in (220 ms); finishes on wheel, off for reduced motion/low resource. Verified on the 38-turn Codex session (header moved 0 px folding and unfolding; 18 → 10 cards on screen). Tests: `apps/ui/test/turn-fold.test.ts` 3, scene membership 1, `tests/browser/turn-fold.spec.ts` 3 (the clamp case failed before the extent hold). Bulk fold/unfold keys shipped with TURN-2 (`c` folds other finished turns, `C` unfolds all); the ten-minute live run is still a MOTION-3 check.
  - Original brief: the history is a custom virtual list (`History.tsx`: measured passage heights, run-based layout, scroll anchors, paged windows with unloaded placeholders), and only the passage that opens a turn knows its turn. Collapsing must keep: scroll anchoring when a header above the viewport collapses; prompt-rail and search jumps into a collapsed turn (auto-expand); selection/expanded operations inside it; Follow and the newest turn (never collapsible while the agent is active); windows that start mid-turn (membership unknown); persisted height estimates. Suggested shape: turn membership computed in the scene composer (as `turnStep` was), collapse state in workspace memory by `turnStep`, collapsed passages rendered as zero-height slots with the header and ledger still visible, plus golden and manual-scroll tests. Accept: collapse/expand never moves the reading position, every jump target auto-expands, ten-minute live run stays calm.

Enforcing the rule needs a forced break before `.op-edit.shape-wide` (the card kind and shape are already classes), a different composition at wide widths. (2) *stacked cards exit by the left edge*: when every card is on its own row (4-card passages at 1024 px, and any passage whose cards are wider than half the row) `flowRoute` tries the left-gutter route before the vertical gap route, so a wire leaves the left edge of the previous card, drops down a gutter 12 px outside the leftmost visible card and enters the next card's left edge; contiguous pairs then read as one long bracket. Sending the wire from the previous card's bottom edge to the next card's top edge would match the contract's wording. No code changed in this slice (the routing lives in non-held `lib/flow-route.ts`; the wrap is CSS), and no real private session was opened: acceptance on three real sessions is a builder-run check using the same spec's measurements.

### HARNESS-AG — Google Antigravity as a third harness (Yash, 13 Nov; built by Codex; research: [antigravity-harness](review/antigravity-harness.md), facts and links in the [ledger](review/04-research-ledger.md#antigravity-google-13-november-2026))

**Branch and release rule (Yash, 13 Nov):** "we'll keep this branch seperate so if its done till releasing properly than we integrate, other wise next version." All AG work merges into the integration branch `codex/harness-antigravity`, never into `main`; each AG item is its own PR *into that branch*. It joins `main` in one reviewed PR only if AG-1 to AG-5 are accepted before Release 1 ships and Yash says so; otherwise it ships in the next version. It must not change anything for Claude Code or Codex sessions. **Why it matters:** Antigravity has been free on student plans, so many students and some professionals use it. **Codex starts from [antigravity-codex-instructions](review/antigravity-codex-instructions.md)**: a paste-ready prompt that sets these boundaries (what it may and may not change, branches, safety, done).

**HARNESS-AG (umbrella; AG-1 to AG-6 are its whole items, and `HARNESS-AG` itself is not an item because `sync-tasks.ts` and `claims.ts` only accept `LETTERS-digits` ids).** Antigravity is a passive, read-only harness: Drishya never starts, steers or approves anything in it (non-negotiables 1 and 2), and the composer and native controls stay unavailable for its sessions.

- **Source decision (measured, review §1, §4, §6).** The primary source is the per-conversation **transcript JSONL** at `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/<conversationId>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl` (fallback `transcript.jsonl`, whose long fields are truncated). It is append-only plain text, so the existing JSONL ingest, byte-offset cursors, generations and tailing apply unchanged. The **session index** is `conversations/../conversation_summaries.db` (all conversations, workspace, parent). The per-conversation **SQLite** file (`conversations/<id>.db`) is enrichment only (AG-6): two thirds of its steps are `CLEARED` and have lost their results. The 21 older `.pb` conversations are **encrypted**; the 12 that have a transcript are still importable through it, the other 9 are reported as unreadable.
- **Existing seams to follow** ([CODEBASE: how to add a harness](CODEBASE.md)): `Adapter` in `packages/capture-core/src/index.ts`; `ToolIntent`/`ToolOutcome` in `capture-core/src/intent.ts`; `seal()` for redaction; `defaultAdapters` in `packages/ingest/src/index.ts`; the Codex adapter (`packages/adapter-codex`) is the closest shape (one JSONL, `discover()` from `listFiles` + `headRecords`).
- **Event-schema impact, exactly** (additive; both builders agree before merge, in the AG-1 or AG-4 PR thread; `CONTRACT_VERSION` and the reducer version move together and the golden scenes are regenerated if 2, 3 or 5 land): (1) `HarnessId` already accepts any string: **no change**. (2) `Observation.correlation.basis` += `'call-order'` (AG-1: a result paired to a call by position in one planner response; the transcript has no call ids). (3) `ImageOmission`/`images[].omitted` += `'referenced-file'`, and, only if Yash agrees, an allow-listed copy of files inside the same `brain/<id>/` (AG-4). (4) none for the model: it stays the captured opaque `MODEL_PLACEHOLDER_Mn`, labelled unresolved. (5) a captured plan-artifact observation for `implementation_plan.md`/`task.md`/`walkthrough.md` with path and version (AG-4), unless the existing plan path carries Markdown. (6) `DiscoveredSource` needs nothing.
- **Redaction before persisting** (review §14), for every item: all content passes `seal()` first. Tool `args` arrive as JSON-encoded strings: decode, then sanitize per key. `touchesProtectedFile` covers `Path`/`AbsolutePath`/`TargetFile`, command lines and patches. **Never read** `daemon/hub_*.json` (CSRF token), `antigravity-browser-profile/`, `%APPDATA%\Antigravity\`, any `mcp_config.json`, `installation_id`, `machineid`; the adapter's allow-list makes them unreachable by construction, including through `file:///` paths in `media[].uri` (real path must resolve inside that conversation's own `brain/<id>/`). Model text (`thinking`, checkpoint summaries) goes through the same sanitizer.
- **Sub-item order:** AG-1 → (AG-2 ‖ AG-3) → AG-4; AG-5 (needs a signed-in Antigravity) can start beside AG-1 and must finish before AG-3, AG-4 and the browser/MCP/question/approval mappings are called done; AG-6 is P1.
- **Shared Accept rules** (each item adds its own): real Antigravity sessions are named by id prefix, size and counts only; screenshots stay in `.local/`; checked at **600 and 1440 px**; the demo bundle and fixtures are regression guards, not acceptance; Claude Code and Codex acceptance does not regress; typecheck, the adapter units and `pnpm check:affected` pass while working, `pnpm check:real -- antigravity` and the targeted specs when done.

Conversations with only an encrypted `.pb` are **counted in a discovery diagnostic** ("N unreadable: encrypted"), never silently dropped and never listed as sessions.
    3. **Read-only database access.** Open only with `file:<path>?mode=ro&immutable=1`; the real `conversations/` folder must end with no `-wal`/`-shm` file that was not there before. Never open a conversation `.db` in AG-1.
    4. **Which transcript, and identity.** Of 44 conversations with both files, 2 differ in length (one has `transcript_full` far shorter). Rule: read both heads, use the one with the larger last `step_index`; mark fields named in `truncated_fields` as partial. The recording identity is the first chosen path and is **sticky**: if `transcript_full.jsonl` appears after a recording was made from `transcript.jsonl`, no second recording is created (test it).
    5. **Parser.** `recordId` = `<conversationId>:<step_index>`; `part` per tool call; a repeated `step_index` replaces (10 exist: confirm the store treats an identical id as a no-op); order by `step_index`, never by `created_at` (it moves back by up to 12 s); `sourceTime` = `created_at` as captured (whole seconds). Map every record type and tool in review §12: reads (`harnessLineNumbers: true`, parse the `N:` rows and the `Showing lines a-b of N` header), edits as `edit {oldString: Target, newString: Replacement}`, writes, searches, `list_dir` as `other` (no directory intent exists: do not invent one), commands, `manage_task` linked to the command by `TaskId`, web, spawn, `send_message` to the existing agent-delivery shape if it fits. `thinking` is stored as `reasoning` (the UI decision is AG-4). `CHECKPOINT` becomes `compaction` with the model-authored summary labelled as such. `SYSTEM_MESSAGE`, `EPHEMERAL_MESSAGE`, `CONVERSATION_HISTORY` are a **named ignore list** with the reason per type in the README; anything else is `unknown`, never dropped.
    6. **Pairing.** Results follow their `PLANNER_RESPONSE` in call order (5,557 of 5,991 planner records have exactly as many results; 434 have fewer; none more). Pair by order and mark `correlation.basis: 'call-order'`; a call with no result is an explicit gap, never a guess; a failed result (`error`, status `ERROR`) is a failed tool result, never success (26% of `edit_file` calls fail with "target content not found").
    7. **Truth rules for results.** A command has an exit status only when the text or a typed field gives one (21 of 2,153 results do): otherwise "no exit status recorded", never pass or fail. A `RUNNING` result in the transcript is never rewritten: show it as started with no recorded result. Output is one stream: do not split stdout and stderr. A write is "tool reported done", not a confirmed after-state.
    8. **Redaction and containment** exactly as the umbrella says; add a test per never-read path that the allow-list rejects it, including a `media[].uri` that resolves outside `brain/<id>/`.
    9. **Capability matrix, honestly:** `readContent` supported, `patchIntent` supported, `confirmedWrite` partial, `beforeSnapshot` and `afterSnapshot` unavailable, `exposedRationale` partial, `delegation` supported, `liveLifecycle` partial until AG-3 proves it, `replay` supported; `testedHarnessVersions` names the two version numbers seen (2.31.2 entry, 44.9.1 `version` file).
    10. **Fixtures:** hand-written, synthetic, in the real shapes: one record of every type and tool in §12, a failed edit, a command that stays `RUNNING`, a repeated step index, a `truncated_fields` record, JSON-encoded arguments, a `.pb`-only conversation, a parent and a child conversation, a record with an image reference, a token canary in an argument, a line cut in half at the end.

### EXPLAIN — IDEA-6, a parked idea (not planned)

**Not being done (Yash, 26 Oct):** kept as one idea, not as work. The notes below are design notes only, with no subtask checkboxes and no implementation claims; the unchecked IDEA-6 umbrella above records its parked status only. Nothing here is built or scheduled.

**Removed under CLEAN-2 (Yash, 12 Nov):** model explanation features (`packages/explain`, model keys and routes) were completely removed from the product. Drishya operates model-free on local captured evidence.

Rules: opt-in per call or per session; preview the exact payload; content redacted at ingest and again before sending; output labelled "Generated by <model>" in a distinct derived style; every claim cites captured evidence IDs (`E1…En`); uncited claims are dropped and counted. This fills the *why* that harnesses no longer record ([123](review/123-harness-data-audit.md) §1). It never replaces captured text and is never presented as the agent's thinking.

- **EXPLAIN-4 · Live validation** · P3 · `explain` — get a Token Factory key; list models (`GET /v1/models`); set real Nano/Super/Ultra ids (defaults in `packages/explain/src/index.ts` are unverified); run explain, briefing and labels on a real session; record latency and cost per call in a short note under docs/review.

- **EXPLAIN-1 · Narrator labels** · P3 · `explain` `live`
  - **Do:**
    - With the narrator on for a session, each burst of consecutive operations (or each passage) gets a one-line generated intent above it, e.g. "Investigating why checkout rejects free orders [E3,E5]", from Nano.
    - Batched, debounced (≥ 10 s or end of passage), cached by evidence hash, cancellable.
    - Shown in the derived style with the model name on hover.
    - The narrator off means zero network calls.
  - **Accept:** works live on a Codex and a Claude session; no call before opt-in; labels cite real operations; cost and latency within the targets recorded in EXPLAIN-4.

- **EXPLAIN-2 · Turn summary and return briefing** · P3 · `explain` `turn`
  - **Do:** on request (button in the turn ledger, or "While you were away" after the tab was hidden for more than 2 min), Super writes 3–5 cited bullets: what changed, what was checked, what failed, and what is still open. Each bullet links to its evidence.

- **EXPLAIN-3 · Explain this hunk (validate)** · P3 · `explain` — the existing Ultra/Super path; validate on real diffs; citations jump to the step.

- **EXPLAIN-5 · Derived visual language** · P3 · `explain` `ui` — one consistent treatment for generated text (for example an italic secondary tint, a small model glyph and a dotted underline on citations) that can never be mistaken for agent prose or captured output, including in exports.

### GIT — Git review, graph and Sacred Timeline

- [x] **GIT-1 · Sacred Timeline** · P1 · `git` `motion` — **finished 20 Oct at the builder's request** (no longer a prototype).
  - **20 October, finished:** branches are grouped into real runs (first-parent walk), each on a seeded side and the nearest free tier, just outside the river's rim (or outside the branch it left), with a slow seeded drift; the river meanders and fades in from older history and out past the newest commit. A strand starts thin and gains width at every commit (new filaments fade in at that commit), never past half the river. Space is black with white stars. Hovering a strand between commits names the branch (its ref), whether and where it merged, where it left, authors, time span and its commits; clicking it opens the newest commit; a commit peek says which commit of its branch it is. Zoom keeps the point under the pointer (buttons: the middle) in the same commit as the new scale, with room added at either end when needed; pinch and Ctrl/⌘ + wheel zoom the graph instead of the page; `+`/`−` keys; buttons step ×1.4 and disable at the limits. Labels of branches below the river sit below it. Tests: `git-light.test.ts` 8; Git atlas spec checks Ctrl + wheel anchoring (< 12 px) and the branch peek. **Not measured:** 60 fps with 500 commits on the reference machine (PERF-1/REL-2); the octopus/shallow/detached matrix is covered by the unchanged topology layout's tests, not re-run visually.
  - **20 October, builder follow-up:** (1) Zoom now magnifies: the river, branch distances and lightning scale with zoom (river 56 px × scale, 14–120 px), where before only the commit spacing grew, so "zoom in" looked like a sideways stretch. (2) Squash merges are shown merging: Git keeps no parent link for a squash, so the daemon now returns content signatures (`git-history.ts` `squashEvidence`: sorted path + written blob, hashed) for each non-merge commit's own change and for each branch head's whole change since it left the primary line; the UI joins them across loaded pages, and a branch whose change a newer primary commit wrote exactly flows into that commit to the right ("squash-merged into main at …" in its peek, with the commit named). No match, no line. On this repository 42 of 65 graph-unmerged branches match. Forks rise early and merges drop late, so a branch travels beside the river and flows in at its merge. Squash-merged heads get no lightning. Tests: daemon squash evidence 1, `git-light.test.ts` 10.
  - **20 October progress:** step 1 prototype landed as [`docs/prototype/sacred-timeline.html`](prototype/sacred-timeline.html) (synthetic topology; WebGL2 additive filaments, half-float bloom, starfield, DOM commits/refs/peek/keyboard; variants River, Ice, Storm; `?lite=1`). Builder looked at the stills and asked to integrate directly and keep iterating toward the reference.
  - **20 October, integrated:** `GitThreadGraph` now draws the light (`lib/git-light.ts` geometry, `lib/light-renderer.ts` WebGL2) under the unchanged DOM commits, labels, peek, paging and keyboard; the SVG ribbon and Orbit remain only without WebGL2. Screen-space and view-bounded: redraws on pan/zoom/hover only (idle 0), ≤ 40k segments (8k and no bloom in low resource). Real-repo fixes: branch commits keep a screen-space clearance from the river at every zoom (buttons use the same positions); lightning size follows the branch's loaded width and only opens forward (builder: flow must read left → right); white-hot core with magenta/violet rims. Verified on this repository (82 refs, squash-merge history). Tests: `git-light.test.ts` 5, Git atlas spec updated for the lit path. **Remaining:** 60 fps measurement with 500 commits, the fork/merge acceptance matrix (octopus, shallow, detached), optional flow motion; the row list is GIT-3. Squash-merged PRs leave no merge edge in Git, so those branches end at their head; GIT-2 is where a session/PR link could show that.
  - Build to the spec in [124](review/124-sacred-timeline-git-graph.md):
    - WebGL/Canvas additive **filaments** with bloom;
    - the main line as a braided luminous river;
    - branches peel off the rim at real forks, rejoin at real merges, and split into lightning tips only at unmerged heads;
    - a starfield backdrop;
    - commit points with DOM buttons, peek and keyboard.
  - Prototype first with the `prototype` skill; builder sign-off on stills before integrating. Topology truth unchanged.

Closing returns to the exact reading place.

- [x] **CONNECT-1 · A prompt to a native session is never cut off by a fixed time limit** · P1 · `controller` `bug` — builder report, 31 October, with a screenshot of the product driving a Claude Code turn: a turn that was still working ended with "harness turn exceeded the connection time limit" and "Turn failed". Cause (31 Oct, Claude, from the code): `apps/daemon/src/controller.ts` arms a fixed 30-minute timer when a run starts and fails the run when it fires, whether or not the harness is still producing output; a long session simply crosses it, and an approval that waits for the person counts against it too. Builder: do not impose a time limit, sessions can be long.
  - **Do:** remove the wall-clock limit on a turn. A turn ends when the harness finishes it, the person interrupts it, or the harness process exits; a harness that is waiting for an approval or an answer is waiting for the person, not failing. Keep the other, short protocol timeouts (a harness that does not answer a control request in 60 s, shutdown after an interrupt), which are about a dead connection and not about a long turn. The explicit `timeoutMs` option stays for tests.
  - [x] **31 October, complete:** the default 30-minute timer is gone; `timeoutMs` stays as an explicit option (tests). A turn ends when the harness finishes, the person interrupts or the process exits; the short protocol timeouts (60 s control answer, 2 s shutdown) are unchanged. Unit: a question left pending past a short window keeps the turn in its approval state and completes when answered, and the same wait with the option set fails with the limit's own message; controller units 23 of 23, connected-workspace and composer specs 26 of 26. Not run: a real native turn longer than 30 minutes (a synthetic harness cannot prove the absence of a clock for that long; the code path that armed it no longer exists).
  - **Accept:** a synthetic native harness that runs longer than the old limit (tested with the limit option set small and with it unset) finishes normally; an interrupt and a process exit still end the run with the right state; an approval pending for longer than any limit does not fail the turn; units for the controller; the existing controller and connected-workspace specs pass.
- [x] **GIT-6 · The Git graph: fast, quieter controls, and branch focus** · P1 · `git` `ui` `perf` — builder request, 31 October: the commit graph keeps its look (the Sacred Timeline texture and colours, black space with white stars) but lags a lot and should be done better and lighter on resources; the buttons around it are not good; and different things can be built around it, starting with selecting a branch and having that branch highlighted.
  - **Measured (31 Oct, Claude, this repository, 50 commits loaded, 1440 px, headless Edge):** the GPU draw is 0.3 ms, but the JavaScript that rebuilds the light geometry runs on every redraw (every pan frame, every pointer move over the graph, every frame of the hover fade): 14 to 20 ms for 52,960 line segments (above the 40,000 the code means to cap at), with spikes of 38 to 51 ms while panning, zooming and hovering. A slower laptop multiplies that. The geometry is rebuilt because it is generated in screen space for the current view.
  - **Do:** (1) **Performance:** build the line geometry once in world coordinates for a tile around the view and reuse it while the view moves (the GPU shifts it); rebuild only when the view leaves the tile, the scale changes or the data changes; make hover and focus a cheap brightness change on the cached geometry, not a rebuild; make the geometry loop itself allocation-free; upload to the GPU only when the data changes; keep the segment cap true. Same picture, measured before and after. (2) **Controls:** the controls around the graph (zoom, fit, orbit, the commit chooser and whatever else is there) become one quiet, aligned group in the product's own style, with nothing that does not earn its place. (3) **Branch focus:** choosing a branch (a strand, a branch in the ref list, or a ref filter) highlights it and dims the rest, shows its fork and merge points, and steps along its commits with previous and next; Escape clears it; the selection survives paging. Topology stays truthful: no invented branches or links.
  - [x] **31 October, complete (Claude):** (1) **Performance:** the light geometry is built once in world coordinates for a tile three viewports wide and reused while the view stays inside it (the GPU shifts it with an offset uniform); it is rebuilt only when the scale or the data changes or the view leaves the tile; hover and focus are a cheap brightness pass on the cached lines (no rebuild); the geometry loop writes into one growing typed array with no per-segment allocation; the renderer uploads lines only when their key changes. Measured on this repository (50 commits loaded, 73,696 segments, 1440 px, headless Edge) before to after: panning 14 to 20 ms of geometry work per frame to 0.1 ms with 0 rebuilds and a worst frame of 49 ms to 5 ms; hover sweep 13.7 ms to 0.0 ms, worst frame 38 ms to 6 ms; zoom steps 18.5 ms to 5.7 ms per rebuild, worst frame 34 ms to 13 ms; drag pan worst 33 ms to 5 ms; frames over 33 ms went from 5 across the four runs to 0. The look is unchanged (same geometry, colours, stars and lightning). (2) **Controls:** zoom, level and fit are one hairline group of icon buttons; the commit chooser and the status line are one slim row at the bottom; the river is centred vertically after Fit instead of sitting low and clipped. (3) **Branch focus:** clicking a strand (or B on a commit) focuses its branch and dims the rest; a chip shows the branch, its place ("2 of 5") with previous and next along its commits, and clear; Escape clears it without closing the dialog; hovering a ref in the list previews its branch; the focused branch's peek offers Open newest commit; stepping selects without leaving the graph. Tests: `git-graph-focus.spec.ts` (2, at 1440 and 700 px: controls, pan without rebuild, focus, step, clear, Escape), affected run (types, units, browsers) green. Not measured: a repository with 500+ loaded commits (this repository has 348 refs and 50 commits loaded per page); low-resource mode was not re-measured (its cap is unchanged and its units pass); not checked on a Codex session's repository (the graph reads the local Git, which both harnesses share).
  - **Accept:** frame time while panning, zooming and hovering at 1440 px on this repository and on a repository with 500+ loaded commits is measured before and after, with the worst frame and the number over 33 ms recorded; the geometry rebuild no longer happens per pan frame; the look is unchanged at rest (before and after screenshots at 600 and 1440 px); low-resource mode still renders; real repositories of both harness sessions (this one and a Codex session's) show branch focus correctly, including a squash-merged branch; keyboard reaches every control; the Git atlas, branches and review specs pass.

  - **Found (31 Oct, Claude, counts only, on this session's own journal `dd136b25`):** a message typed while the agent is working is not a `user` row. Claude Code records it as a `queue-operation` (`enqueue`, with the text) and delivers it into the turn as an `attachment` of type `queued_command` (`prompt` is the text or content blocks with images; `commandMode` is `prompt` for a person's message and `task-notification` for a background task's notice). The Claude adapter ignored that attachment type, so none of them reached the projection: 13 mid-turn messages and 23 task notices in this one journal (8 images among the block-form prompts) were missing, none duplicated as a `user` row. `queue-operation` rows whose message was removed before delivery (2 of 66 enqueues here) have no delivery and are not shown. Fixed in the adapter (below).
  - **Text that arrives late, not reproduced on small sessions:** a real Claude Code run on a throwaway repository (a text, a 20-second foreground command, a text) wrote the first text row 0.8 s before the tool call and 21 s before its result, so the harness does not hold text back until a tool finishes; replaying those real rows into a live recording shows each text within 0.2 to 0.35 s with nothing after it; a synthetic live append (text alone, text after a finished tool, final text) shows within 0.35 s. The large-recording measurement is below.
  - **31 October, part 1 done (adapter):** `queued_command` attachments are now captured as the person's message (text and images, dated when queued); background task notices arrive the same way and are recognised by the existing injected-message reader. Units: 3 adapter tests (text, text and image blocks, empty and other attachments stay ignored); on a tiny journal the projection gains the turn and the live page holds the text (2 matches in the DOM, projection 2 turns). It still arrives at the harness's delivery time, which is the next tool boundary.
  - **Why a typed message still feels late (found 31 Oct):** Claude Code writes the `queue-operation` `enqueue` row with the text the moment it is typed, but only writes the delivering `queued_command` at the next tool boundary, so Drishya shows the message when a tool finishes. Showing it at `enqueue` as a queued message, then delivered when the attachment or the `user` row arrives, and withdrawn if a `remove` row follows (2 of 66 enqueues in this journal were removed undelivered), needs a small projection design (a queued state) and agreement; it is the next step of this item.
  - **Text that is never in the journal (found 31 Oct, live on this session):** visible assistant text written just before a tool call is sometimes absent from the journal altogether. A marker sentence written in a message before a 25-second tool call was not in the journal during the call, nor in the 10 minutes after; three more such texts are also absent, while others from the same session are present as `text` rows (for example the messages before `Merged. Now syncing both trees`). In the journal the missing ones show as `thinking` summaries only (25 messages here have the shape thinking, thinking-summary, tool_use with no text row). No other file under `~/.claude` or the app's folder holds them, and the request blobs hold no assistant text that lacks a row (59 of 59 are present as rows). - [x] **INDEX-1 · The repository map and Files tree skip nested checkouts and agent worktrees** · P1 · `files` `data` `bug` — builder report, 29 October, with a screenshot: the Repository map of the `drishya` workspace is a cube of nodes labelled `.claude/worktrees …` and its footer reads `5000 files · 7496 imports`; the Files tree showed folders that are not in the project.
  - **Cause (read from the code, checked on this machine):** the indexer's walk (`packages/indexer/src/index.ts`, `SKIP_DIRS` plus `.gitignore`) skips `node_modules`, `.git` and the like but not a nested checkout. A Claude Code agent worktree lives inside the project at `.claude/worktrees/<name>/` and is a complete copy of the repository with its own `.git` file; this workspace has 12 of them. The walk descends into every copy, so the 5,000-file cap is reached by duplicates, the real files are cut off, and the map and tree show each file many times. FILES-6 fixed the tree for worktrees *recorded as separate roots* (Codex); it does not cover worktrees *inside* the workspace.
  - **Do:** (1) skip any directory below the root that contains its own `.git` entry (file or directory): it is another checkout (agent worktree, submodule, nested clone), not part of this workspace; apply the same rule in `isIndexableFile` so reading and search agree with the map; (2) bump `INDEXER_VERSION` so cached maps rebuild; (3) when files were skipped for that reason, the map's own status says so ("N nested checkouts not shown"), never silently; (4) the Files tree and the map list the same files.
  - **Accept:** units for a `.git` file, a `.git` directory, a nested checkout under a hidden folder, the root's own `.git` (not a nested checkout) and the file cap not being consumed by a nested copy; on the real `drishya` workspace (12 agent worktrees) the file count, the `truncated` flag and the share of `.claude/worktrees` paths before and after (counts only); the map and the Files tree at 600 and 1440 px on a real Claude Code and a real Codex session of that workspace show only the project's own folders; the map status names the skipped checkouts.
  - **29 October, done:** the walk skips any directory below the root that has its own `.git` entry (file or directory); `isIndexableFile` applies the same rule; `INDEXER_VERSION` is `imports-rules-v2`; the map legend adds "N nested checkouts not shown" (`stats.nestedCheckouts`, optional). Real `drishya` workspace (12 agent worktrees), counts only: before, 5,000 files, 7,496 imports, truncated, 4,999 of the 5,000 paths under `.claude/worktrees`; after, 963 files, 1,498 imports, not truncated, 0 such paths, 12 nested checkouts reported. Real sessions with that workspace: Claude Code `rec_061e` (7,150,502 bytes) and Codex `rec_e77f` (10,412,190 bytes) show 964 map items and 12 nested checkouts at 1440 and 600 px (Claude, in the browser; Codex, map data), and the Files tree names no worktree. Eleven indexer units (four new), `tests/browser/nested-checkouts.spec.ts` at 1440x900 and 600x720, `check:affected` green (460 + 33 browser). **Not covered:** a nested checkout whose `.git` was removed would be indexed as ordinary files; Codex agent worktrees recorded as separate roots stay with FILES-6.

- [x] **DATA-12 · A continued Claude Code session shows its whole history, across the files it continues** · P2 · `data` `content` `home` — builder report, 31 October: "previously we could see the whole history, now it's just up to some particular point, maybe a Claude Code update; shouldn't they be present locally, so we get the whole history instead of just what Claude shows?"
  - **Found (Claude, 31 Oct, counts only):** the history is on disk. The current Drishya working session is journal `dd136b25` (19.7 MB, 29 to 31 Oct, 3 compactions). It does not start at the conversation's start: its first row after metadata is a `compact_boundary` whose `logicalParentUuid` is a message in the earlier journal `c757fa6a` (234.5 MB, 61,002 rows, 19 to 29 Oct), and 277 user/assistant rows (their uuids) appear in both files. Drishya imports each journal as its own recording, so it shows `dd136b25` from its first compaction onward (621 operations, which matches the 622 tool calls the file holds) and nothing of the earlier file; Claude Code's own view does the same. Drishya has no code that reads `logicalParentUuid` or compares uuids between files. Not verified: that this is a Claude Code update (the other 151 local journals were not checked for a split), or how many sessions are affected.
  - **Do:** (1) detect a continuation from the journal itself: a first `compact_boundary` whose `logicalParentUuid` (or whose shared uuids) is found in another local journal; the link is evidence, never inferred from time or folder. (2) Show the lineage as one history: the earlier file's history first, a quiet marker where the continuation begins ("continued from an earlier session file"), then this file, with the earlier part openable on its own. Never copy or rewrite either journal; shared rows are shown once; if the earlier file is gone (retention) say so rather than hide it. (3) Counts, rail, search, Git and replay treat the lineage as one recording or say clearly which part they cover. This is a store and projection question (a recording of several journals), so it needs both builders' agreement and an event-schema note before the change.
  - [x] **1 November, complete (Codex built it, Claude finished and merged):** a Claude journal whose first compaction names a parent message that exactly one other local journal owns is joined to it as one history (earlier file first, a quiet "Continued from an earlier session file" receipt where the continuation begins, with Open earlier part); the join is derived metadata (`session_lineage`, no new observation kind, no rewritten observation), identical shared parts appear once and a differing usage receipt is kept; a missing or ambiguous parent says so and stays independent; unrelated sessions are never linked; native controls, current Files and Git stay on the selected (latest) file. Real pair `c757fa6a` + `dd136b25`: 2 physical parts, 1 exact link, 277 shared message records, 392 identical parts omitted, 1 differing receipt retained, transition at the first retained continuation turn; the receipt shows at 600 and 1440 px (frames in `.local/`, not committed). Local scan of 153 journals: 13 start with a parent reference (5 unique, 5 ambiguous, 3 missing), recorded in `docs/review/data-12-session-lineage.md`. Single-file sessions unchanged (check:real on 3 of them: one part, no join). Units 9, browsers 7 (600/1440, live append, replay boundary, missing/ambiguous).
  - **Accept:** on the real pair above, the full history from 19 Oct to now reads as one lineage with the shared rows once; a continuation whose parent is missing says so; an unrelated session never gets linked; no change to a session that is a single file; live and replay agree; 600 and 1440 px. Check other local journals for the same split first and record the count.

- [ ] **AUDIT-1 · Feature-by-feature product audit before the UI passes** · P2 · `ui` `data` `content` `read` `git` `files` `review` — Yash, 29 October. Many features were added one at a time and some are badly presented or only half handled by the backend: information does not reach the user with ease of navigation, Changes has to be paged through one page at a time (page 256 of 256 on a large session), and the content of some views barely says what it is stating. Features exist; the output and its presentation are not yet a product.
  - **Default state (Yash, 30 Oct):** the audit also lists every surface that starts collapsed or minimized and whether its content should be shown by default (UI-17 is the first example: the plan strip). The tool list added by TOOLS-1 is shown by default too.
  - **Depth (Yash, 5 Nov) — the standard for every audit, UI and motion pass:** explore as a real user doing real work, far deeper than his own ten-minute review (which found eight items). Send prompts and attach screenshots; open every menu and popover; compare each surface with Claude Code, ChatGPT and Codex (chat layout, prompt capsule, composer, menus, tables, diagrams); ask of every fact whether a person would read it; check that every motion opens and closes the same way; try other screen sizes and browser zoom; look at every feature, component, div and pixel against the premium bar. His findings are the floor, not the scope. A pass is not done until it survives this kind of use.
  - **Depth (Yash, 30 Oct):** for every view that has a short form and an expanded form (hover card, row, peek, Inspector, dialog), the audit asks whether everything recorded is reachable (not "N more" with no way to open them), whether the expanded form carries more than the short one and in a shape a person can read (a list, not a block of the harness's raw text), and whether counts say what they count. CONTENT-13 (web search) is the first example found; every other result view is checked the same way, and the UI and motion passes carry the same depth.
  - **User's-eye audit (Yash, 31 Oct):** the audit is also done as a user who has just opened the product: do the files and the Workspace give a path to read the whole file the agent touched, and does every place that shows an edit, a write or a read offer it? A feature that exists but does not help the person (a dead end, an error where an explanation belongs, a view you cannot get out of toward the thing you wanted) is a finding as much as a missing one, and so is anything that would help and is not there. Friendliness is part of the audit, even where it adds a feature.
  - **Interaction inventory (Yash, 31 Oct):** for every component, every kind of interaction it accepts (click, double click, right click, hover, focus and Tab, keyboard, scroll and wheel, drag, Escape, selection and copy, resize), what the product does for each today, on real sessions, in live and replay, loading, empty and failing: written as a file the UI and motion passes use as their checklist. See "The whole-product passes, planned" above.
  - **Scope:** every function that is built today. Inventory first (header and session menu, Home, history cards by action type, prompt rail and turns, live and replay, Files, peek, Workspace, Git review, Git graph, Changes, Search, packets and bookmarks, Inspector, composer and controller, notifications, settings, repository map, and whatever the inventory finds that is not listed here). Yash's own list and any native sessions he supplies are the minimum; do not fit the pass to them.
  - **Method:** use each function as a developer would, on real sessions of both harnesses (small, large and very large), at 600 and 1440 px, live and replay. For each one record: its purpose; what a developer needs there; what is shown; and defects in four places: **UI** (space, layout, controls), **backend** (data not handled to the feature's need, missing or misleading values, slow paths, limits that hide things), **content** (does the text and shape say what it is, in the product's own language), **navigation** (can the user reach what they need without clicking item by item: grouping, filtering, jump, summary before detail, counts that make sense). Also record where it falls short of a product worth $10,000: dynamics, craft, empty/loading/failure states.
  - **Output:** a new file in `docs/review/` with findings by feature, ranked, each with its evidence (session id prefix, counts, a screenshot kept in `.local/`), then whole TASKS items (own IDs, tags, P-level, Accept lines) for every finding, those that block a UI pass first. A feature with no finding still gets its line ("checked, nothing found, on which sessions").
  - **Accept:** every inventory entry has a line; every finding is a TASKS item or an explicit "not doing, because"; the UI pass (UI-8, which absorbs UI-3, UI-4b and UI-7) and the motion passes (MOTION-1, MOTION-3) reference the findings that feed them; the ledger in the [UI pass brief](review/ui-pass-brief.md) has been read first and appended to, and every surface has its written concept answers (brief, section 3); Yash has read the ranked list. The pass finds and plans; it does not fix.

Each assertion was shown to fail with the text forced to the background colour (ratios near 1.0), then restored. The colour walk now reuses one canvas, which took the existing tests from over 50 s (the overlay test timed out at 90 s on this machine) to under 3 s with identical numbers. **Still open:** the 18 px edge veil under the header is deliberately translucent, so cards passing beneath it are not held to a ratio; native `<select>` popups are drawn by the browser; the Paper line-number tone (4.71:1) is the one to watch if that palette is lightened; the human judgement of light-theme balance on Paper cannot be settled by a ratio; real recorded sessions are not covered by the synthetic fixtures.

- [x] **FILES-11 · Current files inside a nested worktree** · P2 · `files` — Yash, 3 Nov (first filed as FILES-10, an ID already used by the Markdown-size setting): a session whose root holds a git worktree under an ignored folder (here `.local/wt-v2`) shows only the lines the agent read, even in Workspace. Cause: the daemon refuses every path under `.local/` (`workspace.ts`, "not a readable workspace path"), and the worktree is not recognised as a root of its own. Proposed: recognise a linked worktree (a `.git` file pointing at this repository, as `repository-root.ts` already does for Home) under the approved root and read it with the same approval, link and protected-path rules; keep refusing `.local/` content that is not a worktree. Accept: on a real session with edits inside such a worktree, Workspace opens the whole current file at 600 and 1440; a plain `.local/` scratch file is still refused with a clear reason. **Done 4 Nov (Claude, PR #342):** a directory whose `.git` is a pointer back to this repository's `.git/worktrees` is read as a root of its own, under the same approval, link, protected-path and redaction rules, with the worktree's own index rules (its `.gitignore`, size, binary). Plain `.local/` files and another repository's worktrees stay refused. A current file the worktree no longer has (it changed branch) answers 404 "the file is no longer there" instead of a raw ENOENT 500, so the reason reads plainly. Checked on this session's own journal (Claude `rec_81c2`, 16k observations, rooted in this repository, with edits in `.local/wt-v2`): the API returns the whole current worktree file (211 lines), a `.local/audit` scratch file is refused with "not a readable workspace path", and Workspace opens the worktree file from disk with no fallback at 1440 and 600. Units: nested-worktree 2/2 plus the workspace suites (85); Workspace and Files specs 44/44. Not covered: images inside a nested worktree (the image reader keeps the `.local/` refusal).

### MOTION — interaction motion

- [x] **MOTION-1 · Every control has purposeful motion** · P2 (final pass) · `motion`
  - **4 November, complete (Claude, PR #345):** every Modern control measured in the running app (what it starts, how long, on which curve) and the gaps filled in one gated sheet (`modern/motion.css`): the Git popover and the reading windows (Git review, Git graph, file readers) open 160 ms and close 120 ms like the menus and dialogs beside them; Workspace arrives where it docks (200 ms); disclosures in menus and Settings settle in place (140 ms, no height); switches on the product curve (160 ms); press feedback eases (120 ms) on every pressable control, four of which had none; dialogs read the one `--ease-out` token. Reduced motion, low resource and keyboard stay immediate. [Inventory, rejected candidates and checks](review/motion-1-inventory.md): Claude `rec_81c2` and Codex `rec_93fd` at 600 and 1440; motion specs 280 passed. Not run: `review-animations`, which only the builder can start.
  - **19 October progress (107):** Short state changes and keyboard/reduced-motion/resource/forced-color checks added; keyboard map focus holds orbit. Modal Escape dismisses immediately with native select behavior. Main prompt/Toolbox/reader motion retained. Full per-control design pass remains open.
  - **Scope:** every clickable control and nested state on every page:
    - prompt minimize/expand, Explorer open/close, Toolbox minimize, folder expand/collapse;
    - Files ⇄ Repository ⇄ Workspace entry and return; menus, popovers, tooltips, settings sections;
    - reading windows and peek tabs; Git drawers; disclosures, toggles and segmented controls;
    - press states; copy feedback; selection.
  - **Rules:**
    - Motion is tied to the true origin and the final state: scale/opacity from the trigger for popovers, translate from the docking edge for panels, a height-free transform for disclosures.
    - Durations are about 120–240 ms with ease-out on enter and a faster exit.
    - Reversals and rapid repeated clicks are interruptible from the current state.
    - No animated virtual-row layout, no delayed or faked content.
    - Reduced-motion and low-resource variants.
  - **Method:** run `find-animation-opportunities` read-only, then `improve-animations` for a plan, implement in small commits, and finish with `review-animations`.
  - **Accept:** an inventory table (control → motion → duration → fallback) committed in a review doc; actual-session desktop checks.

The last two need Harsh and Yash.

- [x] **LIVE-7b · Live steps under 250 ms at every session size** · P0 · `live` `perf` — Yash, 14 Nov, after LIVE-7 (#489) merged: "performance and latency that we have to get the best so no new product is better in terms of backend handling as well ... we cant sacrifice other qualities ... if perf4 can help it later than that, or some mechanism of perf4 can help here as well."
  - **Where LIVE-7 left it** (`docs/review/live-7-latency.md`):
    - Ordinary journals paint live steps at p95 0.16–0.25 s; a new session lists on Home in about 0.5–1 s.
    - Not met: non-paged sessions of about 3,000+ observations stay near 1 s. The daemon rebuilds the whole projection after every update (150–400 ms on its main thread), and "notified → data in page" is the largest stage (p95 514 ms).
    - The tail polls every 100 ms (append → committed p50 60 ms).
    - Not run: live Claude Code (its CLI sign-in had expired), a sub-agent start, a plan (`ExitPlanMode`).
  - **Fable's decision, 14 Nov:**
    1. **An incremental, resumable fold.** The reducer is a fold from the start (see PERF-4), so a followed recording keeps its folded state resident and folds only the new observations. It falls back to a full rebuild when:
       - the journal's revision changes;
       - an observation sorts before the fold's head;
       - `REDUCER_VERSION` changes;
       - a proven parent journal changes.

       Design the folded state as a serialisable, versioned checkpoint: PERF-4's "remembered" checkpoint and its tail-first open are built on this same seam, so the two items share one mechanism rather than two.
    2. **Correctness cannot regress.**
       - A parity test: for the golden scenes and the synthetic and redacted fixtures, folding at every split point equals the full rebuild.
       - A developer-switch runtime check that compares the incremental result's hash with a full rebuild every Nth update and logs a mismatch locally. No telemetry.
       - The composer stays pure: no wall-clock reads.
    3. **An event-driven tail.** A file watch on the followed journal triggers an immediate read, and the 100 ms poll stays as the fallback, only while a viewer listens. Adopt it only if a soak test shows no missed appends and no idle CPU increase.
    4. **The page fetches only what changed** since its cursor, if it does not already; it never re-fetches the whole projection for one step.
    5. **Quality holds:**
       - capture never waits on presentation;
       - no pixel or motion change;
       - memory: one resident fold per followed recording, released when nobody watches;
       - idle CPU unchanged with no viewer.
    6. **Run the missing cases.** Live Claude Code once Yash refreshes the CLI sign-in; a sub-agent start; a plan. While driving controller runs, answer one real question from Drishya on each harness: this is SHIP-5's end-to-end check, which #487 did not run.
  - **Accept:**
    - p95 from append to paint under 250 ms for ordinary journals and under 500 ms for every non-paged size up to the paging threshold. Paged journals under 1 s.
    - Before/after rows added to LIVE-7's table, the same method and machine.
    - The parity test and the runtime check in place; LIVE-7's guards still green.
    - The live, follow and Home specs green.
    - `package:verify` if ingest or store changed.
  - **Done 14 Nov (Claude Sonnet 5.5, PR for Fable's review):** [live-7-latency.md](review/live-7-latency.md) (LIVE-7b section) and [live-7b-fold-checkpoint.md](review/live-7b-fold-checkpoint.md). A resident, versioned fold (`packages/projection/src/fold.ts`) in `Ingestor.projection` with every fallback, parity-tested at every split point and an opt-in `DRISHYA_FOLD_CHECK=N` runtime check; the ordered read happens once; an event-driven tail (file watch, poll as fallback, relaxed with no viewer, released folds) with a soak and idle-CPU measurement. Paired p95 append to paint: small 194 ms, 24 MB Claude 888 to 241 ms, 39 MB Codex 203 ms, 157 MB Claude (paged) 1.01 s to 595 ms; a real live Claude Code run 145 ms. Targets met. "Fetch only the change" measured (30-50 ms with the fold hot) and not built. Missing cases: real sub-agent start (37 ms), a real Claude question answered from Drishya works end to end (SHIP-5's check; nothing to fix); not run: Codex question (not raised without plan collaboration mode), a live plan call, a live Codex sub-agent. PERF-4 persists the fold: the reducer state inventory is in the fold note.
  - **Then PERF-4**, reusing the checkpoint: a cold open of a large session from its checkpoint plus the new tail, the latest page first, and the rest in one quiet batch. PERF-4 also fixes LIVE-7's "stale-open burst": opening a session that already has steps first paints 28 of 52, then catches up about 650 ms later.
- [x] **PERF-4 · Large sessions open at once, the rest loads quietly** · P0 · `perf` `live` `ui` — Yash, 11 Nov, after FILES-15 measured 15.1 s cold / 1.6 s warm on a 185 MB session: "opening the first time as not all at once ... previous some x things and show the whole interface ... and than in background ... in batches rather than one by one ... the user shouldnt know that the thing is updating." Phase F, beside the website. This is the decision PERF-3 was waiting for.
  - **Why it is slow (Opus, 11 Nov, from the code):** the history worker folds every observation of the journal (and a proven parent's) through the reducer before it answers anything that needs the whole session (Files tree, worktree groups, Toolbox counts, Usage, search). Only one complete reducer is resident, so opening another session and coming back is cold again. The reducer is a fold from the start, so the latest prompts cannot simply be reduced on their own with full correctness.
  - **Do:**
    1. **Recent first:** on a cold open of a journal above a threshold (start at about 20 MB, tune on real sessions), build a tail projection of the last N prompts (start at 20, or about 5 MB) and show the whole interface from it within about 1 s. Work that started before the window and facts that need the whole session read as still loading in their own place (a quiet count or a short line in that panel), never as wrong or missing.
    2. **The rest in the background:** the full fold runs in the worker at low priority while the person reads; capture and live updates never wait on it.
    3. **One quiet batch:** when the full projection is ready, swap it in once, not item by item. Operation identities stay the same, the reading position is held exactly (the B2 anchoring), earlier history joins above the view without moving it, whole-session panels update once with no layout jump, and nothing flashes, re-animates or re-runs arrivals. The person should not notice it.
    4. **Remembered:** persist a reducer checkpoint per recording (Drishya-owned data, redacted, versioned by REDUCER_VERSION, invalidated on revision change) so a later cold open resumes from it and appends only the new tail. This makes the second and later opens fast even after a restart or a session switch.
  - **Accept:** on real 150 MB+ sessions of both harnesses (prefix and size only): first full interface under 1 s, complete projection time recorded, zero reading-position movement and no visible re-render at the swap (measured: scroll offset, anchor element, no arrival animation replays), live events during the background fold arrive normally, and the checkpoint makes a cold open after a daemon restart under 2 s. Small sessions behave exactly as today. A unit covers the tail/full identity match and checkpoint invalidation; a browser spec covers the quiet swap at 600 and 1440. Before/after numbers in `docs/review/`, beside LIVE-7's table.
  - **Done 14 Nov (Claude Sonnet 5.5, PR for Fable's review):** [perf-4-cold-open.md](review/perf-4-cold-open.md). Tail projection above 6,000 observations, chunked background fold, one quiet swap, `202 loading` for whole-session endpoints, a persisted fold checkpoint per recording (`folds` in `history/index.db`, invalidated by every input in its key and meta), and the stale-open burst fixed. Real sessions (scratch homes): Claude `dd136b25` 304 MB 4.5 s → 0.84 s first reply, 1.24 s after a restart with 20 new lines; Codex `14a7880a` 959 MB 10.6 s → 0.76 s, 1.52 s after a restart; zero painted movement at the swap at 600 and 1440 on both. Not met as worded: "first full interface under 1 s" is the first reply of the history route (0.76–0.84 s); from navigation to the first painted passage in Edge it was 1.1–2.0 s with the page's own boot. Not done: dock counts and files marks while partial are only handled in the Toolbox and Files; a sub-agent started before the tail; no Antigravity fixture until #490 lands.

The scope grows from showing agent work as text, boxes and animation to the one place a developer uses for any task: Workspace and Files, then Git graph, Changes and branches, with the agent's work shown visually as it happens. Target: millions of people who today only read an agent's text and do not know what it is doing; the product has to feel premium end to end (worth $10,000) and be built so that nobody matches it a day later in agent visualization and developer ease at one place.
